THE MECHANISM: a 4th q8_1 activation layout `GGML_CUDA_Q8_1_PQ2` — qs bytes permuted INSIDE each 16-element group (position k*4+m holds element m*4+k) + half2 (d, int16 sum) per 32-block → `(code_word >> 2k) & 0x03030303` pairs RAW 2-bit codes with activation bytes so **dp4a needs NO per-weight decode** (one shift+mask yields four dp4a-ready bytes) and the ternary digit bias is ONE integer subtraction via the ACTIVATION's stored sum — 'sum(q*(c-1)) = sum(q*c) - sum(q)' (qsum already lives in block_q8_1; the correction rides existing data, no per-weight (c-1) decode); warp = 4 rows reusing the activation slice (PQ2_0_MC_ROWS 4 × PQ2_0_MC_WARPS 4); `__launch_bounds__(128, 3)` register cap = 3 blocks/SM; admission predicate: Ada-only (`cc == GGML_CUDA_CC_ADA_LOVELACE`), plain 2D, no ids, not batch-invariant, 3-8 columns, per-ncols template switch 3..8. the PERF content is the chescock review thread 2026-09-21, applied by the author in-PR)** — THE LAW: a runtime bool branching around a large inlinable body forces the inliner into duplicate-or-deinline; restructure as a CONDITIONAL GUARD so the body compiles ONCE. chescock verbatim: 'Normally the system and `get_param` all get inlined together, but since `self.is_exclusive` is a runtime check this will have to either inline *two* copies of the system, or leave it as a non-inlined function call.' + the fix sketch verbatim: 'let _guard = self.is_exclusive.then(|| { // Set `world.last_change_tick` and create a guard to set it back }); run(world)?; — That should ensure that `run` is only compiled in once.' + the author's confirmation verbatim: 'Yep that works pretty well, changed!'. The merged shape: `if self.is_exclusive { world.last_change_tick_scope(last_run, run) } else { run(world) }` (two copies of the inlinable `run` closure) → the `LastTickGuard` + single `run` call. Not a quantization issue: the same file is right on CPU'; the fix: 'The fusion check (ggml_metal_mul_mat_add_operand) already selects the operand by identity; make the encoder do the same'; the test: 'Add a MUL_MAT_ADD mode to test-backend-ops where the residual is a second mat-mul; on Metal it fails 27 of 28 cases before this change'. The law: a fusion admission that must pick ONE operand of a binary op selects by BUFFER IDENTITY, never by a negated op-predicate — negation is sound only when at most one operand can match. **LEAD 2 — llama.cpp `d2a79e6046` #29171 sycl GLM MLA prefill MKL FA admission — A− (PREFILL), kernel_opt D**: 'GLM-4.7 Flash uses an MLA shape with 576-wide Q/K heads, a 512-wide V head, GQA 20, and F16 KV. The SYCL dispatcher rejects this shape because the normal MKL flash-attention gate requires matching K/V widths and caps the head dimension at 512, so prompt processing falls back to the substantially slower TILE kernel'; the fix admits ONLY the validated exact shape ('Admit only the validated 576/576/512, GQA-20 F16 shape to the existing MKL pipeline. Keep all other mismatched K/V shapes on their current fallback paths') + the V-cache-as-strided-view-of-K aliasing conditions ('Handle GLM's V cache as a narrower strided view of K rows. Select the strided F16 descriptor when row stride is padded, and alias K/V dequantization buffers only when their logical widths match. Restrict the stride exception to a real V view sharing K's row stride') + 'Add the exact 576/512, GQA-20 prompt-path backend test. On an Intel Arc Pro B70 at master e613ef2, pp8192 improves from 432.80 to 1292.29 tok/s (2.99x, +198.6%). tg256 remains unchanged within noise at 45.67 versus 45.65 tok/s'; ⚠ IN-COMMIT NEGATIVE: the F16-scores half ('store MKL flash attention scores in F16 ... The F32 score matrix is the largest flash-attention intermediate on this path; storing it as F16 halves its size and traffic ... pp8192 1583.9 -> 1657.1 (+4.6%) pp64000 610.0 -> 684.5 (+12.2%) pp131072 ~354 -> 402.1 (+13.5%)') was REVERTED within the same merged PR ('Revert "sycl: store MKL flash attention scores in F16" — This reverts commit 265f9748164dc88e02678425b75f520ccd528b32' — revert reason unstated in-commit; measured-positive on record, landed-reverted — watch-only, never stage the F16 half without the revert reason). **LEAD 3 — llama.cpp `988190680d` #30065 metal few-row MMA remaining src0 types — B−, RIDER on fewrow-tensor-tile-decode-into-rhs-operand (B198; the B205 a3a1c47 precedent: upstream few-row MMA instances are dedup-notes, not rules)**: 'The generic few-row MMA kernel works for any type with a 16-weight dequantizer, so it now also takes BF16, Q1_0, Q2_0, MXFP4, Q2_K, Q3_K, TQ2_0 and the IQ types. Each type starts at the row count where it beats the current kernels on an M3 Ultra: 5 rows for TQ2_0, 4 for BF16, 3 for MXFP4, Q2_0, Q2_K and IQ4_NL, and 2 for the others' + the measured table ('0.23 to 0.98 from the threshold to 8 rows, 0.24 to 0.33 at 9 to 16 rows, and 0.99 to 1.01 at 1 and 512 rows', M3 Ultra source-reported, interleaved mean-of-two) — the PER-TYPE row-threshold table + the 16-weight-dequantizer generality condition are the new decision content; rider, not a rule. **LEAD 4 — llama.cpp `b9acf138a1` #29928 GLM5Next MTP headless forward — B−, kernel_opt D (own rule or rider at conversion)**: 'A NextN forward with no output rows (the MTP catch-up and the draft-context prefill) persists only through its cache writes, so the headless graph keeps the MLA latent, indexer key|gate and pooled-key writes and drops the query path, the indexer selection, the attention body, the FFN and the LM head. The 4-token catch-up falls from 6.9 ms to 0.33 ms of kernels' — then the LANDED final state CROPS instead of prunes: 'Replace the headless NextN prune with the crop pattern the other MTP graphs use: gather the attention output and the block input at the output ids before the position-wise FFN and the shared head. A NextN forward with no output rows then runs the FFN and the head over zero rows. The 4-token catch-up falls from 6.9 ms to 2.9 ms of kernels; greedy output hashes are unchanged' — the consistency-over-deep-prune tradeoff IN-COMMIT measured on both sides (0.33 prune vs 2.9 crop; crop landed for the shared pattern) + 'the greedy output hashes and the draft acceptance are unchanged' correctness pins; also the trunk/MTP-only GGUF split-load + the shared-tail rollback refusal (partial recurrent rollback whose tail cell is shared is REJECTED, never silently moved). **LEAD 5 — cubecl `9865237018` #1770 parallel compilation — C+/B−, RIDER on profile-window-drains-queued-work-before-open (B207, the drain family) + a work-stealing note**: the patch (4826 insertions/2437 deletions, full .patch read) splits compilation into KernelLoader + ArtifactCompiler (any-thread lowering) / CompilationTarget (server-thread load/store) with parallel batch compilation ('The half of the work any thread can do then runs on as many threads as there are kernels, up to the configured parallelism ... However long the queue, it compiles in one phase that lasts about as long as its slowest kernel'); the drain-family member is the measurement honesty — client.rs: 'Compile every kernel queued for compilation ..., before the work submitted after it. Every measurement starts with it — a tune, a throughput probe — so a queue a compile-only dry run left is not timed as part of the first launch measured' + tuner.rs: 'Kernels a compile-only dry run queued compile here, together, before anything is timed: left to the next launch, they would all compile inside the first candidate's warmup, and its measurement would carry every candidate's compilation'; the work-stealing law: 'kernels differ by orders of magnitude in how long they take to compile, so a fixed split would leave threads idle behind the slowest share' (for_each_at_once dynamic next-index pickup, first-panic-resumed); > **(1) `fewrow-tensor-tile-decode-into-rhs-operand` A− (lead `3285e757a` #284/#286; few-row approach adapted from the Yukon MLX.fast qmm_m16_block core, Apache-2.0 — provenance in-source): a cooperative-tensor matmul2d tile of 16 src1 rows × 32 weight rows per simdgroup at speculative verify widths, PQ2_0 weights DECODED STRAIGHT INTO the right-hand cooperative tensor ("so the K loop needs no threadgroup staging and no barriers"), because both neighbors lose — "the generic tensor mul_mm tile is 64 x 128, which computes far more columns than a 4..16 row verify batch needs, and mul_mv_ext is ALU-bound at 2..8 columns" (ops.cpp, in-source). Measured THREE-neighbor crossover with the gaps named: mat-vec < 3 | fewrow 3..32 | generic tensor > 32; "n=1..2 are slower on the new tile (150 vs 71 and 130 us), hence min 3" (commit message, M5 Pro best-of-2: n=8 484.5→157.4, n=16 855.9→172.8, n=32 864→328.3 µs @ 17408×5120). NCB×NKS template split (5 pinned host combos, `GGML_METAL_PQ2_0_FEWROW_CFG`), kill-switch `FEWROW=0`, and the tensor element-layout PROBE technique ("The cooperative tensor element layout was probed on M5 with get_multidimensional_index before writing the decode"). End-to-end (M5 Pro, 27B PQ2_0, DFlash2 drafter, greedy depth 7): speculation 0.73× plain → **1.57× (BF16 drafter) / 1.64× (Q8_0 drafter)** with the route on; lossy disclosure in-source ("Output matches plain decoding except one near-tie (top-2 logprob gap 0.00047) where the half-precision activations pick the other token" — the Issue-750-T3 class, honestly carried). (2) `weight-decode-amortized-across-output-columns` B (same commit, mul_mv `_nc` kernel): "The generic mul_mv_ext path re-decodes every weight per column. > **RULE LEADS (A− 4):** (1) `grammar-window-draft-prune-path-local-mask` — structured-output × DFlash2-class drafting: before the verify forward the grammar matcher walks the draft tree depth-first (`accept_token` down, `rollback` up), drops drafts NO accepted path can hold (a grammar-rejected token, a stop token, and every row under either — "the parent's masked row could never choose it"), fills each kept row's allowed-token bitmask from its path, −inf's the rest before sampling; EXACTNESS LAW row-locality: "A row's logits do not depend on the other rows, and its mask depends only on its path, so a drafted row and the serial step with the same path choose the same token, greedy or keyed" (98fb8f9, at-sha — the line moved files); bounded refusal surface (xgrammar missing / schema won't compile / TP rank-1 structured-off with packed-constraint sharing "both ranks then walk and mask the same rows", grammar.py:158); NOVEL — zero grammar/constrained-decoding content in corpus (the 8 "grammar" grep hits are B193's "name grammar" prose + proposer parser comments). (2) `affine-block-address-lets-pipeliner-fly` — layout-for-addressability: the stored-block address `(b // 4) * 32 * SBN + ((b % 4) * 16) // 2 * SBN` is a STAIRCASE in the loop step — "an address the pipeliner cannot see as affine is not put in flight" (443ad4a, at-sha) — and since a stored macro block holds 8 tile rows the same address is simply `b * (8 * SBN)`: "8 bytes a block (low nibble: even input), 8 * SBN apart: an address the pipeliner follows" (nvfp4.py:312, in-source); distinct-from hoist-bit-address-out-of-packed-row-sweep (LICM hoists an INVARIANT; this makes an ADVANCING address affine so the pipeliner overlaps it); the priority datum "the weight traffic is what the step actually waits on" (message-only). (3) `register-width-topk-falls-back-to-tiled-radix-select` — per-row selection holding `next_pow2(blocks)` scores in registers hits a SPILL cliff (`SELECT_REGS = 32768  # _select holds a row's block scores in registers up to this many; past it they spill`, attention.py:13 in-source; past it a 256-row prompt block read 26-27 ms vs 0.8 — message-only); the fix dispatches by register budget with an OUTPUT-IDENTICAL fallback: "``_select`` while ``blocks`` fit its registers, ``_select_tiles`` (the same lists) past them" (attention.py:353 in-source) — tiled radix select one byte a pass from the top, ties by lowest block id; distinct-from wide-verify-warp-specialized-scores-in-registers-pv (that CHOOSES the registers posture by width; this handles the spill side with an identical-output fallback). (4) `startup-estimate-derived-from-allocation-shapes` — the capacity plan's EXL3 term was a fixed 1.125 GiB/rank guess while the real engine allocated an `exl3_mm.Scratch` per buffer set + 1 GiB prompt split-K partials NOTHING counted → ~2.5 GiB under-count, admission lied; the fix derives every term from the allocation shapes themselves (`exl3_expert_scratch` mirrors `Scratch`'s formula, geometry.py:222-227 in-source) and proves it with a FAKE-ALLOCATOR RECONCILIATION: at real shapes, allocated ≤ estimate (3f35ea9); absorbs the estimator-blindness facets: the NVFP4 widening made byte-count estimates blind 4.00×→1.01× (3655360, message-only) and the `--checkpoint-slots` knob joins the estimate per kept state + is refused pre-download when unservable (f151c6f, serve_options.py:36 in-source) — `cache-slot-knob-budget-counted-refused-pre-download` folds here as a sub-arm; NOVEL (the 2 "reconcil/ALLOCATION SHAPE" grep hits are pad-skip prose + a B-correction note, not this mechanism).
> **B+ (8):** (5) `prefix-cache-entry-one-token-early` — a kept prompt-state entry ending AT the prompt's last token can never hit: a resume needs a STRICTLY SHORTER prefix (an identical resend, or a next turn whose final token renders differently — thinking on/off) always prefills in full; keep the entry at `max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` with a MID-CHUNK state cut (per-layer DeltaNet state + conv window captured between two launches, `Cut` forward.py:123 + `return max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` decode.py:121, both in-source) + MTP tail rows after the point; resume-equals-fresh pinned ("(``resume`` equals a fresh run); ``e.kept`` resumes prompt[:keep_at]", decode.py:316); NOVEL. (6) `dual-reduction-shared-butterfly-ladder` — the serial DeltaNet recurrence computes TWO dependent lane-sums per step (state mem + next node's read-out); BOTH ride ONE `__shfl_xor` butterfly pass — "Each node's read-out sum goes through the next node's butterfly (same order), so a step waits on one, not two." (gdn.cu:143, in-source); bit-safe (identical xor ladder); refuse when the piggybacked sum feeds the SAME step's update (true dependency); DIRECTLY relevant to our GDN chunked lanes; NOVEL (the "piggyback" grep hits are the dirty-bit ENCODING rule — different mechanism). (7) `idle-rank-doorbell-on-rendezvous-store` — idle TP rank 1 waited INSIDE the all-gather (NCCL kernel spinning + host copy thread spinning; "96% GPU util / ~210% CPU on two DGX Sparks" message-only); the fix: rank 0 sets a key in the rendezvous TCPStore NCCL ALREADY HOLDS before each request header ("the idle doorbell: rank 1 waits for each request on the rendezvous store (no store: no doorbell), not in NCCL", engine.py:295 in-source; `store.wait([key], timedelta(hours=1))` :314, consumed keys deleted, 1-hour timeout retries, other store errors raise, no-store skips silently); kin deadline-bounded-poll-of-submitted-completion-event (block-not-spin); NOVEL (the reuse-existing-store doorbell is the content). (8) `prompt-item-per-consumer-kernel-measured` — grouped-MoE routing item width is occupancy-vs-L2-reread and BOTH directions lose (~1.5-1.6× when mis-set, message-only): general grouped kernel keeps 64 pairs/item ("16 ran 1.64x slower on Flash Next's routed prompts", experts.py:14 in-source), the NVFP4 grouped kernel takes 16 ("64 ran 1.53x slower", nvfp4/experts.py:13 in-source), and the plan buffer is sized at the NARROWEST consumer ("TILE = 16 … the narrowest a prompt plan's consumer may take", experts.py:13); the pinned trap "the ceiling and the count are easy to confuse, and only the count is traffic" (9933492, at-sha comment later replaced); kin the measured-cutoff family (register-prefetch-ring-measured-cutoff, the xwide n-floor lesson — per-kernel measured constants, never one shared constant). (9) `splitk-reduce-one-launch-slice-order-pinned` — a split-K fp32 face reduced by `out.copy_(part[0]); for s: out += part[s]` burns one elementwise launch per slice (31 of 32 wasted); both faces share ONE `_reduce` kernel with the SUMMATION ORDER PINNED: "The adds are the loop's own, in the loop's order, one fp32 add a slice, so the fp32 sums are bit-identical to the loop's" (443ad4a message; TIP `_reduce` docstring in-source) pinned by `test_matmul_splitk_sum_order_is_the_reduces_one`; split-k family EXTENSION — the in-kernel spellings ride as instances (qmm.cu:177 "K slices of a tile form a cluster: slice 0 adds peers' partials in slice order, as reduce_kernel does" + :202 `cluster.sync()` "peers keep their memory until slice 0 has read it", sm_90-gated `__trap()` below; exl3 linear.cu:244-246 last-block-done atomicAdd election reducing Z in split order with `__threadfence()` + `__ldcg`). (10) `declared-uncapturable-expert-declines-graphs` — a step whose dispatch reads host state (the NVFP4 grouped expert's plan `.tolist()` — a synchronizing D2H) cannot run in a capture and surfaced AFTER weight load as "operation failed due to a previous error during capture" with nothing pointing at the cause — INVISIBLE to the capture tests, which ran EMPTY plans where tolist() reads nothing (the lane-intel gem); the fix: the expert class DECLARES `capturable = False`, the engine reads the declaration, declines graphs with one loud line, decodes eagerly — "experts that read their plan on the host (NVFP4) can't be captured: decline graphs before a capture fails" (decode.py:142-144 in-source); later flipped True when the plan moved device-side (64e2374, `capturable = True     # the plan stays on the device` nvfp4_moe.py:30) — the DECLARATION is the capability axis; NOVEL. (11) `concurrent-round-parity-solo-equal` — `--parallel N, each reply equal to its solo run` (7399e7c) carried by (a) batch-invariant kernels — "rows run the same groups and K slices at any row count, so no row affects another" (qmm.py:1 in-source; Nemotron twin "A row's logits have the same bits at any row count (the row count is a runtime value)" sources.py:177) — an INSTANCE of batch-composition-invariant-grid-early-return B100; (b) the one-stream engine's stopping rule reused per stream (scheduling leg NOVEL); (c) admission counts N streams' states+caches BEFORE load and refuses with the window that fits; test matrix (16 streams, mixed lengths, greedy+keyed, drafts on/off, join/leave) message-only. (12) `quantized-stored-bytes-not-widened-patterns` — DOWNGRADED from the read agent's A−: decode-from-wire-bytes is our corpus's AMBIENT default (every q4/q8 kernel), so the GOAT framing fails; the mineable content is the widened-pattern ANTI-PATTERN (their NVFP4 table stored one uint16 bf16 pattern per weight = 4.00× checkpoint bytes; the fix holds the wire bytes — "True: the checkpoint's bytes (codes + fp8 scales), decoded in-kernel" nvfp4.py:34 in-source, "the device holds what the file holds (four times less than a widened grid)" message-only) + the field-arithmetic decode identity ("the pattern is a bf16's bits, so the bitcast is exact" :314 in-source) + the estimator facet → rider on lead 4; distinct-from B176 (licenses EXACT widening into a compute dtype — different axis: kernel-path widening vs STORAGE-form widening) + fp4-nibble-gather-lut-replicated-index-width (the decode-mechanism choice INSIDE the kernel — complementary).
> **B (6):** (13) `draft-context-ring-window-bounded` — drafter KV in a ring of `window + block + tile − 1` rows ("``ring`` (TF_GLM_DRAFT_RING): the context in a ring of the window and block, the same drafts." dflash2.py:178 + "0: a flat buffer, logical row p at p" :259, both in-source) — the block pass reads exactly the rows it always read; TWO admission-critical riders: the capacity ESTIMATOR must count the ring not capacity ("counted two copies" → "20 GiB estimated at a 1,048,576 window" vs a 21.25 MiB ring, message-only) and any snapshot/kept-state across ring writes copies the WINDOW rows first (kept-prompt ring-snapshot contract glm5_next decode.py:388-392: keep the window while `R - point < drafter.ring - drafter.window`, else drop the drafter resume rather than keep a torn window); distinct-from ring-overwrite-gated-k-back-readiness B194 (STAGE ring ≠ CONTEXT ring); NOVEL. - [x] **P45 — mlxfast-bonsai2 `Hadamard.swift` post-`cfb35b0d`: per-shape CACHED zero-pad buffers + the leading-rows module-slot. GRADED 2026-10-09 (M3 idle, zero-cargo pin read — Hadamard.swift fetched at main tip; ercumentyildirim `6e19fe1` named in-source): YES ANNOTATION-GRADE RIDER — the pad-materialization THREE-RUNG LADDER completes the B195 pad facet.** The corpus owns both poles — B195's rider on entries_gpu_51 owns the ELIMINATION pole ('the zero rows never exist as a buffer', pad-in-read, the BF16 drafter-head path, THIS same file) and B84/B186 own the per-call/tile staging economics; `cachedNarrowZeros` is the unowned MIDDLE rung: the zero rows exist EXACTLY ONCE per (rows, cols, dtype) shape key (NSLock-guarded dict), shared across every verify-width call site (the narrow route pads rotated+sums, the int8 route pads the input, the head route pads headInput), safe because write-never-BY-CONTRACT. Rider decision contents: (1) SCOPING — the cache serves only the verify-window paths that repeat every speculative round per projection; the sub-threshold matrix-route pad in the SAME function still allocates fresh zeros (the cache goes where repetition lives, not where padding exists); (2) bounded WHOLESALE eviction (>12 → removeAll keepingCapacity) — zeros cost nothing to rebuild, LRU bookkeeping is overkill for constant buffers (contrast B103's evict-oldest negative table: verdicts are expensive to recompute, zeros are not); (3) the ladder ordering fold-into-read > once-resident-shared > per-call. Secondary atom: `LeadingRowsSlot` — a derived sub-module (weight[0..<rows] views under the same transform) cached per row count and held OFF the module tree as a plain class ('never loaded, updated or counted as a parameter'; a block drafter scores a frequency-ranked vocab prefix through it) — the same off-the-reflection-tree discipline as `HadamardConstantLayoutCache`/`matrixRoute` in the same file. NotBenchable (arena submission, no numbers); kill-switch `MLXFAST_RIDER_NARROW_ZERO_PAD`. Lane intel stands: the Issue-985 dense-ab register-tile class (cache resident pad/aux buffers per shape). QUEUED as the third item of the cargo-quiet conversion batch (Leads 1+2; quotes re-verified at the pin at conversion time).

CLOSED 2026-10-09: LANE N/A + CORPUS-OWNED (the expected mostly-dedup, confirmed total).** Our FWHT never launches standalone: the K1-K5 fused rotation kernels fold the butterfly INTO the consuming kernel at the folded file's 1024-wide block (Bench 940's addendum — butterfly + per-16 quantize inside the kernel; split-path fallbacks only behind non-1024 geometry guards), so the upstream standalone-kernel width ladder (warp ≤512 / block 1024-8192, the 512-1023 CUDA hole) has NO surface to land on in our lane. Width regime gate: 'Widths 64 to 512 keep the warp kernel. 1024 through 8192 use the new one, for both F32 and F16 sources.' Dispatch clause (secondary, own half): the shared admits-op predicate is a deliberate SUPERSET of kernel coverage with a safe fall-through — 'ggml_cuda_op_mul_mat_use_fwht ... does not check width, so it needed no change here: any width it admits that ggml_cuda_op_fwht can't serve already falls through correctly to the cuBLAS path.' > **(2) bevy `a144d280` / #26042 "Specify EntityLocation alignment to improve codegen" (merged 10-07T19:33Z, NEW past the B210 pin) — rust_perf candidate, B+ — the store-to-load-forwarding stall law.** A small struct's field alignment decides LLVM's register partition on the copy: since #25632 EntityLocation is written 8+4+4 and the first 8-byte chunk joins archetype_row with HALF of the second register — 'LLVM builds it by writing both registers to the stack and reading 8 bytes back. That read spans two pending stores and causes the store-to-load forwarding to fail.' The fix is ONE alignment attribute tipping the partition to 4+4+8 so the join happens in-register: 'Adding a `repr(align(8))` to `EntityLocation` seems to tip LLVM codegen the right way.' Measured: world_entity/50000 233.33 → 66.48 µs (x0.26); get_entity_mut_slice/20 152.1 → 100.9 ns (the 1.49× regression to parity). DEDUP: grep-verified NO rust_perf rule owns store-to-load forwarding / register-partition alignment (the hits are B207's inline-wrapper rule + the vec-layout/buffer-reuse/repr(C)-copy trio — different mechanisms). FAMILY PRECEDENT LANDS SAME DAY: #26040 (same author, the unmarked-wrapper inline loss) converted as B207's rust_perf rule THIS MORNING — the second member makes it a family. IN-LANE: any hot struct crossing an aggregate store to a partial-field read — riir-games-shared/KAT store types are reachable; the enum-of-fields padding law is the sibling concept (alignment as a PARTITION choice, not just size).
